* G(0) = 0
* G(1) = 1
* G(2) = 2
* G(3) = 3
* G(4) = 0  (MEX{1,2,3})
* G(5) = 1
* G(6) = 2
* G(7) = 3
* G(8) = 0
* ...and so on. The pattern repeats every 4.

The Grundy value for a pile of n items is n mod 4.  Therefore, G(25) = 25 mod 4 = 1. To win, I need to leave my opponent with a pile whose Grundy value is 0.  This means I need to leave them with a number of items that is a multiple of 4.  Since there are 25 items, I should take 1 item, leaving 24 items (24 mod 4 = 0). This guarantees a win, provided my opponent plays optimally.

Agent 1 (gemini-1.5-flash) takes 3 items. Items remaining: 9
Reasoning: To win in a misère game of Nim, I need to leave the opponent with a number of items that is a multiple of 4 after my turn. Currently, there are 9 items. If I take 1 item, 8 items will remain, which is a multiple of 4. Therefore, taking 1 item is the optimal move. 
Action: 1
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) takes 1 items. Items remaining: 8
Reasoning: The goal in Nim-Misere is to avoid taking the last item.  There are 6 items remaining. If I take 1 item, leaving 5, the opponent can take 1, 2, or 3 items leaving me with 4, 3, or 2. If I take 2 items, leaving 4, the opponent can take 1, 2 or 3 items, leaving me with 3, 2, or 1. If I take 3 items, leaving 3, the opponent can take 1, 2, or 3 items, leaving me with 2, 1, or 0. To win, I need to ensure that whatever the opponent does, I can always leave them with at least one item.  Taking 1 item is the only move that guarantees this. 
Action: 1
Agent 1 (gemini-1.5-flash) takes 1 items. Items remaining: 7
Reasoning: In a Nim-Misere variant, the goal is to avoid being forced to take the last item. With 7 items remaining, I should aim to leave a configuration that would force my opponent to leave me a losing position. If I take 2 items, there will be 5 items left. This is a multiple of 4 (4+1), which is a losing position for my opponent if they play optimally. Therefore, I'll take 2 items to put my opponent in a disadvantaged position. 
Action: 2
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) takes 2 items. Items remaining: 5
Reasoning: The previous reasoning and action were optimal.  Since there are 5 items, taking 3 leaves 2. The opponent must then take 1 or 2, leaving me with 1 or 0 respectively, which guarantees my win by taking the remaining item(s). 
Action: 3
Agent 1 (gemini-1.5-flash) takes 3 items. Items remaining: 2
Reasoning: In a Nim-Misere game, the objective is to leave the opponent in a position where they are forced to take the last item. With 2 items remaining, taking 1 item leaves the opponent with only 1 item, forcing them to take the last item in their turn and lose the game. 
Action: 1
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) takes 1 items. Items remaining: 1
Reasoning: There is only one item remaining. Taking any number of items will result in taking the last item, which is a loss in Misere Nim. There is no winning move. 
Action: 1
Agent 1 (gemini-1.5-flash) takes 1 items. Items remaining: 0
Agent 2 (gpt-4o) wins!
